ℹ️
Tip for exams: Read the question carefully to identify (1) what type of outcome (continuous/binary), (2) how many DVs, (3) whether groups are compared, (4) whether there is clustering. This tells you which topic's code to use.
1️⃣

T1 — MANOVA

▼
💡
Change: your_file.csv · IV (grouping variable, e.g. HelmetType) · DV1, DV2 (continuous outcomes, e.g. BrainDamageIndex, ConsciousLevel)
R — T1 MANOVA
# ── 0. Load packages ─────────────────────────────────────────
library(tidyverse); library(car); library(biotools); library(emmeans)

# ── 1. Load data ──────────────────────────────────────────────
data <- read.csv(""your_file.csv"")           # or read_sav("file.sav")

# ── 2. Descriptive stats ──────────────────────────────────────
data |> group_by(IV) |>
  summarise(across(c(DV1, DV2), list(mean = mean, sd = sd)))

# ── 3. Check DV correlation (must be 0.3–0.9) ────────────────
cor(data[, c("DV1", "DV2")])

# ── 4. Box's M test (homogeneity of covariance) ──────────────
boxM(data[, c("DV1", "DV2")], data$IV)   # p>0.05 = assumption met

# ── 5. Fit MANOVA ─────────────────────────────────────────────
model <- manova(cbind(DV1, DV2) ~ IV, data = data)
summary(model, test = "Wilks")             # Wilks' Lambda (primary)
summary(model, test = "Pillai")            # Use if Box's M is significant

# ── 6. Univariate follow-up (Bonferroni α = 0.05/k) ─────────
summary.aov(model)                          # F per DV; use α = 0.025 for 2 DVs

# ── 7. Post-hoc pairwise (if IV has ≥3 groups) ───────────────
emmeans(model, ~ IV) |>
  contrast("pairwise", adjust = "bonferroni") # adjusted means + 95% CI
2️⃣

T2 — MANCOVA

▼
💡
Change: covariate (e.g. ExposureScore, PreTestScore) · IV (e.g. DonorGroup) · DV1, DV2 (e.g. AttitudeScore, FeelingScore)
R — T2 MANCOVA
# ── 0. Packages ───────────────────────────────────────────────
library(tidyverse); library(car); library(biotools); library(emmeans)

# ── 1. Load data ──────────────────────────────────────────────
data <- read.csv(""your_file.csv"")

# ── 2. Check covariate–DV correlation (r > 0.3 required) ─────
cor(data[, c("covariate", "DV1", "DV2")])

# ── 3. Check homogeneity of regression slopes (interaction test)
model_int <- manova(cbind(DV1, DV2) ~ IV * covariate, data = data)
summary(model_int, test = "Pillai")       # IV:covariate p > 0.05 required ✓

# ── 4. Fit MANCOVA ────────────────────────────────────────────
model <- manova(cbind(DV1, DV2) ~ IV + covariate, data = data)
summary(model, test = "Pillai")          # use Pillai if Box's M significant
summary(model, test = "Wilks")           # Wilks' Lambda if Box's M OK

# ── 5. Univariate ANCOVA follow-up ───────────────────────────
summary.aov(model)                         # Bonferroni-corrected α = 0.05/k

# ── 6. Adjusted means (EMM) ──────────────────────────────────
emmeans(model, ~ IV, multivariate = FALSE) |>
  contrast("pairwise", adjust = "bonferroni")
3️⃣

T3 — Data Visualisation (ggplot2)

▼
💡
Change: categorical_var, group_var, predictor, outcome, IV, continuous_var → all must exactly match column names in your dataset (case-sensitive)
R — T3 Data Visualisation (ggplot2)
library(tidyverse)
data <- read.csv(""your_file.csv"")

# Bar chart ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
ggplot(data, aes(x = categorical_var, fill = group_var)) +
  geom_bar(position = "dodge") +
  labs(title = ""Your Title"", x = ""X Label"", y = "Count") +
  theme_bw()

# Scatter + regression line ────────────────────────────────────
ggplot(data, aes(x = predictor, y = outcome)) +
  geom_point() +
  geom_smooth(method = "lm", se = TRUE, colour = "blue") +
  theme_bw()

# Boxplot by group ─────────────────────────────────────────────
ggplot(data, aes(x = IV, y = outcome, fill = IV)) +
  geom_boxplot() +
  theme_bw() + theme(legend.position = "none")

# Histogram ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
ggplot(data, aes(x = continuous_var)) +
  geom_histogram(bins = 30, fill = "steelblue", colour = "white") +
  theme_bw()

# Save plot ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
ggsave(""plot_name.png"", width = 8, height = 6, dpi = 300)
5️⃣

T5 — Correlated / Clustered Data (LMM + GEE)

▼
💡
Change: outcome · predictor1, predictor2 · cluster_id (school/district/patient ID) · time (for random slope) · exchangeable (correlation structure) · family: gaussian() for continuous, binomial() for binary
R — T5 Correlated Data (LMM + GEE)
library(lme4); library(lmerTest); library(broom.mixed); library(performance)
data <- read.csv(""your_file.csv"")     # must be LONG format

# ── Step 1: Null model — check if clustering matters (ICC) ────
m0 <- lmer(outcome ~ 1 + (1 | cluster_id), data = data, REML = TRUE)
icc(m0)                  # ICC > 0.05 (5%) → clustering matters → use LMM

# ── Step 2: Random intercept model ────────────────────────────
m1 <- lmer(outcome ~ predictor1 + predictor2 + (1 | cluster_id),
            data = data, REML = TRUE)
summary(m1)
tidy(m1, conf.int = TRUE, effects = "fixed")   # fixed effects table with 95% CI
r2(m1)                   # marginal R² (fixed only) + conditional R² (fixed+random)

# ── Step 3: Random slope model (if time is a predictor) ───────
m2 <- lmer(outcome ~ predictor1 + predictor2 + (1 + time | cluster_id),
            data = data, REML = TRUE)

# ── Step 4: Model comparison (REML = FALSE required for LRT) ──
m1_ml <- update(m1, REML = FALSE)
m2_ml <- update(m2, REML = FALSE)
anova(m1_ml, m2_ml)      # p < 0.05 → retain more complex model

# ── GEE alternative (population-averaged effects) ─────────────
library(geepack)
gee <- geeglm(outcome ~ predictor1 + predictor2,
               id = cluster_id, data = data,
               family = gaussian(),          # gaussian=continuous; binomial=binary
               corstr = "exchangeable")  # exchangeable / ar1 / unstructured
tidy(gee, conf.int = TRUE)
6️⃣

T6 — Machine Learning Regression

▼
💡
Change: your_file.csv · outcome (your continuous response column name) · 2G (RAM allocation for H2O) · 50, 50 (hidden layer nodes) · 100 (epochs). The . in lm(outcome ~ .) means use ALL other columns as predictors — remove unwanted columns first.
R — T6 Machine Learning Regression
library(Metrics)
data <- read.csv(""your_file.csv"")

# ── Train / test split (70:30) ────────────────────────────────
set.seed(123)
ind   <- sample(nrow(data), nrow(data) * 0.7)
train <- data[ind, ];   test <- data[-ind, ]

# ══ Model 1: Linear Regression ════════════════════════════════
lm_mod <- lm(outcome ~ ., data = train)      # "." = all other columns as predictors
summary(lm_mod)
rmse(test$outcome, predict(lm_mod, test))      # lower RMSE = better

# ══ Model 2: Decision Tree Regression ═════════════════════════
library(rpart); library(rpart.plot)
dt <- rpart(outcome ~ ., data = train, method = "anova")
printcp(dt)                                           # find row with min xerror
dt_pruned <- prune(dt, cp = dt$cptable[which.min(dt$cptable[, "xerror"]), "CP"])
rpart.plot(dt_pruned)
rmse(test$outcome, predict(dt_pruned, test))

# ══ Model 3: Neural Network (H2O) ═════════════════════════════
library(h2o)
h2o.init(nthreads = -1, max_mem_size = ""2G"")     # set to ≤50% of your RAM
train_h <- as.h2o(train);   test_h <- as.h2o(test)
nn <- h2o.deeplearning(
  x = setdiff(names(train), "outcome"),
  y = "outcome",
  training_frame = train_h,
  hidden = c(50, 50),                    # hidden layer nodes (adjust)
  epochs = 100                                  # training passes
)
h2o.performance(nn, newdata = test_h)
h2o.shutdown(prompt = FALSE)

# ── Compare all 3 RMSE values ─────────────────────────────────
# Linear RMSE vs Tree RMSE vs NN RMSE → pick lowest
7️⃣

T7 — Factor Analysis (EFA → CFA)

▼
💡
Change: 2 (n factors from parallel analysis) · oblimin (rotation: oblimin if factors correlated, varimax if independent) · item1, item2... (your actual item column names) · Factor1, Factor2 (your factor labels) · ML (estimator: ML for normal data, MLR for non-normal, WLSMV for ordinal)
R — T7 Factor Analysis (EFA → CFA)
# ══ EFA ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
library(psych); library(GPArotation)
data <- read.csv(""your_items_file.csv"")     # items only — no ID column

# Step 1: Adequacy checks ──────────────────────────────────────
KMO(data)                      # Overall MSA ≥ 0.60; each item ≥ 0.50
cortest.bartlett(data)         # p < 0.05 required

# Step 2: Number of factors ───────────────────────────────────
fa.parallel(data, fm = "pa", fa = "fa")   # parallel analysis (recommended)

# Step 3: Run EFA ─────────────────────────────────────────────
efa <- fa(data,
          nfactors = 2,             # from parallel analysis
          rotate   = "oblimin",      # oblimin (correlated) or varimax (orthogonal)
          fm       = "pa")                  # Principal Axis Factoring
print(efa, digits = 3, cut = 0.32, sort = TRUE)  # cut = loading threshold

# Step 4: Reliability per factor ──────────────────────────────
alpha(data[, c("item1", "item2", "item3")])  # α ≥ 0.70 needed

# ══ CFA ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
library(lavaan)
cfa_model <- '
  Factor1 =~ item1 + item2 + item3
  Factor2 =~ item4 + item5 + item6
'
fit <- cfa(cfa_model, data = data, estimator = "ML")
summary(fit, fit.measures = TRUE, standardized = TRUE)
# Fit criteria: CFI ≥ 0.95 · TLI ≥ 0.95 · RMSEA ≤ 0.06 · SRMR ≤ 0.08
8️⃣

T8 — Structural Equation Modelling (SEM)

▼
💡
Change: Latent1, Latent2 (your latent variable names) · item1...item6 (indicator items) · outcome (observed endogenous variable) · predictor (exogenous variable) · ML (estimator: ML/MLR/WLSMV) · Remove the mediation block if not testing mediation
R — T8 Structural Equation Modelling (SEM)
library(lavaan); library(semPlot)
data <- read.csv(""your_file.csv"")

# ── Define model ──────────────────────────────────────────────
sem_model <- '
  # Measurement model (latent variables from CFA)
  Latent1 =~ item1 + item2 + item3
  Latent2 =~ item4 + item5 + item6

  # Structural paths (directional hypotheses)
  Latent2  ~ Latent1           # Latent1 predicts Latent2
  outcome  ~ Latent1 + Latent2  # both predict outcome

  # Mediation (optional — define indirect effect)
  a := Latent1 ~ predictor
  b := Latent2 ~ Latent1
  indirect := a * b
'

# ── Fit SEM ───────────────────────────────────────────────────
fit <- sem(sem_model, data = data, estimator = "ML")
summary(fit, fit.measures = TRUE, standardized = TRUE, rsquare = TRUE)

# ── Fit indices to report ─────────────────────────────────────
fitMeasures(fit, c("chisq", "df", "pvalue", "cfi", "tli",
                   "rmsea", "rmsea.ci.lower", "rmsea.ci.upper", "srmr"))
# Good fit: CFI/TLI ≥ 0.95 · RMSEA ≤ 0.06 (90% CI) · SRMR ≤ 0.08

# ── Modification indices (if fit is poor) ────────────────────
modindices(fit, sort = TRUE, maximum.number = 10)  # modify with theory only!

# ── Path diagram ──────────────────────────────────────────────
semPaths(fit, what = "std", layout = "tree", edge.label.cex = 0.8)
⚙️

REAAAPP Workflow

Standard Epidemiological Analysis Framework · GET503 · DrPH USM

REAAAPP is the structured analysis workflow taught at USM DrPH. Each letter represents a mandatory step — skipping any step compromises validity or reproducibility of results.

R E A A A P P
R

Research Question & Study Design

  • State the research question clearly (PICO/PECO framework)
  • Identify the outcome variable and its measurement level (continuous, categorical, time-to-event)
  • Identify predictors / covariates / confounders
  • State the study design (cross-sectional, cohort, RCT, quasi-experimental)
  • Determine appropriate statistical method based on outcome type and design
Key question: What is your outcome? How many groups? Is data correlated/clustered?
E

Exploratory Data Analysis (EDA)

  • Inspect data structure: str(data), head(data), summary(data)
  • Check for missing values: colSums(is.na(data))
  • Descriptive statistics: mean, SD, median, IQR, frequency tables
  • Visualise distributions: histograms, boxplots, bar charts
  • Check for outliers: boxplots, z-scores, Mahalanobis distance
  • Explore bivariate relationships: correlation matrix, scatter plots
library(skimr); skim(data)
library(DataExplorer); plot_missing(data)
library(corrplot); corrplot(cor(data[,num_vars]))
A

Assumptions Checking

Verify all statistical assumptions before running the inferential model:

Test/MethodAssumption CheckedThreshold
Shapiro-Wilk / Q-Q plotNormalityp > 0.05 (normal)
Levene's testHomogeneity of variancep > 0.05
Box's M testHomogeneity of covariance (MANOVA)p > 0.001
VIF < 10 / Tolerance > 0.1No multicollinearityVIF < 5 preferred
Durbin-WatsonIndependence of residuals~2.0
KMO > 0.6, Bartlett's p < 0.05Factorability (EFA)KMO > 0.8 meritorious
ICC (LMM)Clustering / need for multilevel modelICC > 0.05 justifies LMM
A

Analysis — Run the Statistical Model

ScenarioMethodR function
Multiple continuous DVs, 1 IVMANOVAmanova()
MANOVA + covariateMANCOVAManova() {car}
Continuous DV, clustered dataLMMlmer() {lme4}
Latent factors from itemsEFA → CFAfa() / cfa() {lavaan}
Causal / mediation pathsSEMsem() {lavaan}
Continuous DV prediction (ML)Decision Tree / ANNrpart() / neuralnet()
Visualise patternsData Visualisationggplot2
A

Accuracy / Model Assessment

  • Regression / LMM: R², Adjusted R², RMSE, residual plots (fitted vs residual, Q-Q)
  • MANOVA/MANCOVA: Wilks' Λ, Pillai's trace, η² (partial eta-squared), observed power
  • EFA: % variance explained, factor loadings ≥ 0.40, Cronbach's α ≥ 0.70
  • CFA/SEM: CFI ≥ 0.95, TLI ≥ 0.95, RMSEA ≤ 0.08, SRMR ≤ 0.08
  • LMM: ICC, AIC/BIC comparison (RI vs RS), likelihood ratio test
  • ML Regression: RMSE, MAE, R² on test set; cross-validation
P

Presentation of Results

  • Present descriptive statistics first (Table 1: demographics/baseline)
  • Report inferential results in structured tables (MJMSTable / gtsummary / flextable)
  • Always report: effect size, 95% CI, exact p-value (not just p < 0.05)
  • MANOVA: report Wilks' Λ, F, df, p, η² — then follow-up ANOVA per DV
  • LMM: table with fixed effects (β, SE, t, p) and random effects (variance, ICC)
  • SEM/CFA: path diagram + fit indices table
  • Figures: properly labelled, captioned, saved at ≥ 300 DPI
library(gtsummary)
tbl_summary(data, by=group) |> add_p() |> add_overall()
# Export
library(flextable); as_flex_table(tbl) |> save_as_docx(path="table1.docx")
P

Publication / Reporting Standards

  • Follow MJMS (Malaysian Journal of Medical Sciences) formatting for tables and references
  • Report statistical methods in Methods section: software version, packages used, significance threshold (α = 0.05)
  • Interpret results in context — distinguish statistical significance from clinical/practical significance
  • Avoid overclaiming causation from cross-sectional/observational data
  • Include limitations: sample size, design constraints, unmeasured confounders
  • CONSORT (RCT), STROBE (observational), PRISMA (systematic review) reporting checklists where applicable
Write-up template: "Data were analysed using R version [x.x.x]. [Method] was performed to assess [objective]. Statistical significance was set at α = 0.05. Results are presented as mean ± SD [or median (IQR)] and [β/OR/F] with 95% CI."

⚡ Essential One-Liners

# Check R version
R.version.string

# See loaded packages
search()

# Check if package installed
"lavaan" %in% rownames(installed.packages())

# Update all packages
update.packages(ask=FALSE)

# SPSS file
data <- haven::read_sav("file.sav"); data <- haven::zap_labels(data)

# Quick descriptives
psych::describe(data)

# Correlation matrix
round(cor(data[,sapply(data,is.numeric)], use="complete.obs"), 2)

# Missing values summary
colSums(is.na(data)); round(colMeans(is.na(data))*100, 1)
